#+begin_src python
import bibtexparser


with open('tmp.bib') as bibtex_file:
    bib_database = bibtexparser.load(bibtex_file)

print(bib_database.entries[0])

myorg=[
    bib_database.entries[0]['title']+"\n",
    ":PROPERTIES:\n",
    "EXPORT_AUTHOR: "+bib_database.entries[0]['author']+"\n",
    "EXPORT_FILE_NAME: index.md\n",
    ":EXPORT_HUGO_SECTION_FRAG: "+bib_database.entries[0]['ID']+"\n",
    "END",
    "\n",
    "#+begin_src toml :front_matter_extra t",
    'publication_types= ["2"]',
    'publishDate='+bib_database.entries[0]['year'],
    'publication='+bib_database.entries[0]['journal']+"\n",
    "selected=true",
    "#+end_src"
]
#+end_src
*** parse bibtex in elisp
#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(defun hash-table-keys (hash-table)
  (let ((keys ()))
    (maphash (lambda (k v) (push k keys)) hash-table)
    keys))


(defun rmsym (mystr)
  (string-replace "}" "" (string-replace "{" ""
					 (string-replace " and" "," mystr)
					 )
		  )
  )





(defun import-paper (&optional str)
  "import bibtex paper on clipboard or from argument to ox-hugo

STR is current-kill if unspecified.
"
  (interactive )
  (setq str (or str
                (current-kill 0)))
  (insert (with-temp-buffer
            (insert str)
	    (setf ht (parsebib-collect-bib-entries))
	    (setq mykey (car (hash-table-keys ht)))
	    ;; create path if necessary
	    (setq mypath (concat "content/publication/" mykey))
	    (if (file-directory-p mypath)
		nil
	      (make-directory mypath)
	      )
	    (write-region nil nil (concat mypath "/cite.bib"))
	    (setq ht2  (gethash mykey ht))
	    (setq aut (rmsym (cdr (assoc "author" ht2))))
	    (erase-buffer)
	    (setq mytitle (rmsym (cdr (assoc "title" ht2))))
	    (setq myyear (rmsym (cdr (assoc "year" ht2))))
	    (setq mymonth (rmsym (cdr (assoc "month" ht2))))
	    (setq myjour (rmsym (cdr (assoc "journal" ht2))))
	    (setq myurl (rmsym (cdr (assoc "url" ht2))))
	    (rmsym mytitle)
	    (insert (concat "TODO " mytitle "\n"))
	    (insert ":PROPERTIES:\n")
	    (insert (concat ":EXPORT_AUTHOR: " aut "\n"))
	    (insert ":EXPORT_FILE_NAME: index.md\n")
	    (insert (concat ":EXPORT_HUGO_SECTION_FRAG: " mykey "\n"))
	    (insert (concat ":EXPORT_DATE: " myyear "-" mymonth "-01" "\n"))
	    (insert ":END:\n\n")
	    (insert "#+begin_src toml :front_matter_extra t\n")
	    (insert "publication_types= [\"2\"]\n")
	    (insert (concat "publishDate=\"" myyear "\"\n"))
	    (insert (concat "publication=\"" myjour "\"\n"))
	    (insert "math=true\n")
	    (insert (concat "url_pdf=\"" myurl "\"\n"))
	    (insert "selected=true\n#+end_src")
            (buffer-string)))
  )



(setf ht (parsebib-collect-bib-entries))

(cdr (assoc "url" ht2)
(setq mykey (car (hash-table-keys ht)))
(eval mykey)

(eval ht)
(gethash mykey ht)

(setq ht2  (gethash mykey ht))
(setq aut (cdr (assoc "author" ht2)))
(setq aut (string-replace "}" "" (string-replace "{" ""
		(string-replace " and" "," aut)
		)
		)
)


(concat "EXPORT_AUTHOR: " aut)

(gethash "author" ht2)

(setq ht (parsebib-parse))

(parsebib-read-entry)

(eval ht)

(hash-table-p ht2)

(setf hash (make-hash-table :test #'equal))
(puthash "a" 1 hash)
(gethash "a" hash)

@article{hayete_identification_2021,
  title = {Identification of {{Latent Variables From Graphical Model Residuals}}},
  author = {Hayete, Boris and Gruber, Fred and Decker, Anna and Yan, Raymond},
  year = {2021},
  month = jan,
  journal = {arXiv:2101.02332 [cs, q-bio, stat]},
  eprint = {2101.02332},
  eprinttype = {arxiv},
  primaryclass = {cs, q-bio, stat},
  url = {http://arxiv.org/abs/2101.02332},
  urldate = {2021-02-08},
  abstract = {Graph-based causal discovery methods aim to capture conditional independencies consistent with the observed data and differentiate causal relationships from indirect or induced ones. Successful construction of graphical models of data depends on the assumption of causal sufficiency: that is, that all confounding variables are measured. When this assumption is not met, learned graphical structures may become arbitrarily incorrect and effects implied by such models may be wrongly attributed, carry the wrong magnitude, or mis-represent direction of correlation. Wide application of graphical models to increasingly less curated "big data" draws renewed attention to the unobserved confounder problem. We present a novel method that aims to control for the latent space when estimating a DAG by iteratively deriving proxies for the latent space from the residuals of the inferred model. Under mild assumptions, our method improves structural inference of Gaussian graphical models and enhances identifiability of the causal effect. In addition, when the model is being used to predict outcomes, it un-confounds the coefficients on the parents of the outcomes and leads to improved predictive performance when out-of-sample regime is very different from the training data. We show that any improvement of prediction of an outcome is intrinsically capped and cannot rise beyond a certain limit as compared to the confounded model. We extend our methodology beyond GGMs to ordinal variables and nonlinear cases. Our R package provides both PCA and autoencoder implementations of the methodology, suitable for GGMs with some guarantees and for better performance in general cases but without such guarantees.},
  archiveprefix = {arXiv},
  keywords = {Computer Science - Machine Learning,Quantitative Biology - Quantitative Methods,Statistics - Machine Learning},
  file = {C\:\\Users\\fgrub\\Zotero\\storage\\QYMKMZVE\\Hayete et al_2021_Identification of Latent Variables From Graphical Model Residuals.pdf;C\:\\Users\\fgrub\\Zotero\\storage\\2H2UAVXQ\\2101.html}
}
#+end_src

    - CLOSING NOTE [2020-06-28 Sun 18:12]
Hola Lucian o,
Soy estudiante de Matías de guitarra en el tango, y él me recomendó que te contactara.
Quería saber si ofreces classes por internet de canto (especialmente en el contexto de tango).
Mi objetivo es poder cantar y acompañarme en la guitarra al menos para poder practicar en casa.
No tengo ningun entrenamiento en canto anteriormente.

Gracias
Fred

I can make a script that runs in a directory and:
- generates thumbnail
- extract title from metadata
- generate script to update gallery to a text file

#+begin_src html

<!-- nanogallery2 portable - http://nano.gallery -->
<div id="ngy2p" data-nanogallery2-portable='{"itemsBaseURL":"http://nanogallery2.nanostudio.org/samples/","thumbnailWidth":"200","thumbnailAlignment":"center"}'>nanogallery2
  <a href="berlin1.jpg" data-ngthumb="berlin1t.jpg" data-ngdesc="">Berlin 1</a>
  <a href="berlin2.jpg" data-ngthumb="berlin2t.jpg" data-ngdesc="">Berlin 2</a>
  <a href="berlin3.jpg" data-ngthumb="berlin3t.jpg" data-ngdesc="">Berlin 3</a>
</div>
<script> var st = document.createElement('script'); st.type = 'text/javascript'; st.src = '//nano.gallery/portable.php?k=m&u='+encodeURI(window.location.href); document.getElementsByTagName('head')[0].appendChild(st); </script>
<noscript>Please enable javascript to view the <a href="//nano.gallery">gallery powered by nanogallery2.</a></noscript>
<!-- end nanogallery2 -->
#+end_src

** Try pcalg
#+BEGIN_SRC R
  library("pcalg")

  data("gmG")
  gmG8$g %>% plot

  suffStat <- list(C = cor(gmG8$x), n = nrow(gmG8$x))

  pc.gmG <- pc(suffStat, indepTest = gaussCItest,
	       p = ncol(gmG8$x), alpha = 0.01)

  dev.new()
  plot(pc.gmG)

  data("gmL")
  suffStat1 <- list(C = cor(gmL$x), n = nrow(gmL$x))
  pag.est <- rfci(suffStat1, indepTest = gaussCItest,
		  p = ncol(gmL$x), alpha = 0.01, labels = as.character(2:5))

  dev.new()
  ll
  plot(pag.est)
#+END_SRC

   - CLOSING NOTE [2014-09-28 Sun 13:06] \\
     finished review last night. submitted it
*** Review
**** Summary
This paper presents 3 distance metrices between spatial fields based
on mathematical morphology concepts of erosion and dilation. THey also
present the connection between the different metrices as well as some
general properties of interest.
The authors also present an index to rank a set of spatial fields
based on their similarity
**** Typos and suggestions
- page 1 spatial is repeated in first paragraph, second column
- page 5 crossreference is missing.
- page 6 last paragraph crossreference has a typo
- page 7 the paragraph before table 5 is not clear. Seem to be missing
  something in the grammar.
- fig 3 has very low resolution. need to be higher
- table 6-8 difficult to read. font is too small
- page 8 line 40 matrixes=matrices
- Section 5 title mention classification but in the discussion of this
  section I don't see any actual classification algorithm. I would
  expect to see a way to group the fields in a specific number of classes.
**** Conclusions
It would have been useful to have, in the case study, an example of
the advantages of using these metrics over other metrics. What does
these metrics provide that other metrics do not.
The conclusion of the paper actually mention an intersting
appliclation for classification which would seem like a good
example. I wished they had implemented this in the paper.
****

**
** Analyzing rare variants like copy numbers is a big issue
ways to deal with this can be high impact
** biological multiresolution analysis
in signal processing we talk about analysing signals at different resolutions by using for example wavelet transforms.
this decompositions happens in terms for time and frequency which are the main characteristics of the signals that we deal with.

I wonder if there is an analogous  type of multiresolution analysis of biological significance that we can create.

is there a math behind this? Aanlougus to fourier or wavelet transforms

*** Activar opciones de desarrollador
En el fire stick ir a:
- "Settings",
- luego seleccionar "System"
- seleccionar Developer Options
- activar "ADB Debugging" (ponerlo en "ON")
- Regresar presionando el boton the regresar en el control
*** Buscar el IP del firestick
- Ir a "System"
- Seleccionar "About"
- seleccionar "Network"
- Copiar el IP
*** Copiar el kodi en el fire  stick
En la computadora ejecutar el AdbFire
** Delcamp Guitar forum
http://www.guitarraclasicadelcamp.com/
*** Introduction
Presentación: latinoamerica, vals, tango, milonga, flamenco

Hola a todos,

Me pueden llamar Federico y tengo 37 años. Soy originario de Panamá pero ahora vivo en Estados Unidos. Practico en una guitarra clásica Yamaha CGX171CCA.

He sido aficionado de la guitarra clásica desde la secundaria en donde empecé a tomar classes privadas y con el método de Carcasi. Luego durante mis estudios de ingeniería en la universidad tomé clases en una escuela de música por unos meses y luego continué estudiando classes privadas con un estudiante mas avanzado que conocí allí. Él me introdujo a la serie de libros de la escuela razonada de la guitarra clásica de Pujol y en esta temporada que practicaba mucho llegue hasta empezar el tercer libro.

Luego de este period, empecé estudios graduados en ingenieria asi tuve que reducir mi practica de guitarra considerablemente. Más que nada me pase practicando solos para guitarra classica de musica española (como Lagrima, Adelita, Canarios, etc), latinoamericanas (valses de Antonio Lauro, Villalobos, etc) y arreglos de musica popular (como música de los Beatles).

En este periodo me involucre en un club de Tango argentino y me volví aficionado del baile y la música argentina, especialmente el tango, los valses y la milonga. De esta manera descubrí que la guitarra en el tango es fascinante en termino de las tecnicas que se emplean, la combinacion con otros instrumentos y los guitarristas que cantan al mismo tiempo que tocan arreglos en la guitarra.

Actualmente estoy practicando algunos tangos en solo y tambien uno arreglo de un tango llamado Mariposita en donde la guitarra hace el acompañamiento y la melodia es algún otro instrumento o voz (por ejemplo https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mHTlaN-aY6Q). Espero tocar con un colega que sabe tocar violin y si nos sale bien tal vez consiga que un amigo que canta opera que cante pero ya veremos. La parte de la guitarra es bien complicada.

 Mis goles actuales son:
- aprender mas teoría musica, especialmente armonia y poder hacer mis propios arreglos a partir de melodias o de arreglos para otros instrumentos como la piano.
- aprender mas tangos y tocar con otros instrumentos
- tocar y cantar. Al menos tango. Pero esto lo veo dificil
- flamenco. Me encanta flamenco clasico al estilo de Paco Peña. Me gustaria aprender alguna de sus composiciones. Empece con Herencia Latina, pero hay otras que me gustaria aprender.
- mejorar mi tecnica:
  + regresar a practicar a partir del segundo libro de Pujol
  + practicar tremolo
  + practicar rasgueo de flamenco
  + practicar armonicos
*** entonacion
recomendación de tutorial para entonación
Hola,
estoy tratando de aprender ha escribir acompañamientos de guitarra para  tangos
y  lo que he encontrado dificil es poder cantar la melodia especialmente al mismo
tiempo que toco el acompañamiento ya que este último no tiene la melodia, solo armonias.

Podría alguien recomendarme recursos para:
- mejorar la entonacion y poder cantar las notas
- mejorar la coordinacion de voz cantando la melodia y acompañamiento en la guitarra.

muchas gracias
Fred

*** Introduction
I worked and lived with 3 different families in a span of 3 years, one of the families are authors and would travel a lot for business so I was there to help with their son, to cover when they were tied up. They were the first family I lived with, and their child was a gregarious 4-year-old boy with a megawatt smile. For the second multicultural family, which is comprised of three ethnically diverse adopted children and one biological miracle baby, I took care of their four children ages 12-4, two boys and two girls. The third family was a newborn. I bottle fed the infant, burped, bathed, did tummy time, changed diapers and helped during the teething phase. The children ages 4-12 had similar activities with regards to their morning routine, I did school drop-offs, then returned to the residences to do light housekeeping. ** Car loan
#+begin_src R
T=30000 ## total  loan you need to pay back
i=4/100 ## annual rate
N=60 ## payments per month

prin=T/60 ## principal paid every month

library(tidyverse)
tibble(Payment=1:N,
       Principal=prin,
       Total=T,
       Principal_Paid=cumsum(c(0,Principal[-1])),
       Balance=Total-Principal_Paid,
       Interest=i/12*Balance
       ) %>%
  mutate(TotalInterest=sum(Interest)) %>% tail


intermonth## interest per month
#+end_src

** Tango guitar
Hello,
I'm an amateur classical guitarrist and tango enthusiast from Boston.
Got into Argentine Tango initially from dance lessons but just love the music as
well and I'm looking to learn more about tango guitar.

I may be going to New York for a few days in September(18-20) and I was wondering if
whether you are available this days and the cost of lessons.

I'm specially interested in getting some guidance on how to write my own
guitar tango arrangements specially
- for guitar solo
- for guitar plus voice or some other melodic instrument like violin
- guitar + singing? I imagine if the same person that plays the guitar sings
 then the arrangment has to be simpler than if
 a different person is doing the singing? Are they tips or guidance for this?

I'm thinking starting from either a piano score (like what you can get for todotango.com) or
perhaps get a guitar solo and convert it to guitar+voice.

thanks
Fred

Hola Carlos
Son un aficionado de la guitarra classical y del tango (musica y baile) y estoy
interesado en aprender mas de la guitarra en el tango.

Posiblemente estaré viajando de Boston a NYC en Septiembre (18-20) y me gustaría saber si estas
disponible y el costo de tus lecciones.

Estoy interesado en una introduccion a escribir arreglos de tango para la guitarra
especialmente:
- para guitarra solista
- guitarra + voz o algun otro instrumento melodico como violin o flauta
- guitarra y canto. Me imagino que si la misma persona que toca
la guitarra canta entonce los arreglos tienen que ser mas sencillos?

Pienso usar como referencia version de tango para piano como lo que se puede conseguir in todotango.com o convertir solos de guitarra tango a guitarra+voz o guitarra+canto.


Gracias
Fred

** COnfiguring emacs

Based on the comments, I got Xwidgets and Cairo to work. Here's what I did:

Install gtk3 libraries: sudo apt-get install libgtk-3-dev libwebkitgtk-3.0-dev

Configure with ./configure --with-cairo --with-xwidgets --with-x-toolkit=gtk3

** Unison asking for password in windows
The Key is located at c:/Users/fred/.ssh
make sure the public key that I'm using is in the server .ssh/authorized_keys file

In the last problem I had the public key was bad. So I had to:
- regenerate the key using msys2 console with ssh-keygen
- I generated a new key without password
- copied the public key to server's .ssh/authorized_keys

*** 2018-07 July
**** 2018-07-01 Sunday
***** Cortando la Hierba Y escuchando sobre stoicismo
Hoy corte la hierba. No estuvo tan mal excepto por los incesantes insectos.   Para entretenerme estuve escuchando un libro sobre estoicismo. Lo más interesante fue escuchar sobre el valor de mantener un diario y mantener notas sobre lo que sucedio el dia anterior. Aparentemente distinguidos pensadores como Marcos Aurelios y otros hacían esto. El libro esta interesante y pienso q debo conseguir el ebook para usar la didactica que recomiendan de leer differentes fragmentos cortos por dia.

Otros temas interesantes que mencionaron:
- distinguir lo que se puede cambar de lo que no se puede cambiar.
- aprender a decir ~no~ a todo aquello que no es importante

He decido tratar de escribir algo todos los días. La pregunta es
- Si usar orgmode o onenote
- a mano o imprenta
- español o inglés

Por ahora probare en español y en orgmode.
**** 2018-07-02 Monday
***** Obs
Tengo q hacer una lista de o. para ver si puedo mejorar las cosas. Esta muy dificil todos los días tener que hacer la interminable lista de repeticiones. No creo poder aguantar mucho más. Las cosas tienen que cambiar. Especialmente las diarias.
*** 2018-09 September
**** 2018-09-11 Tuesday
***** Notas del viaje a Panama

****** vuelo
Esta vez volamos de Boston a Panamá en Copa y nos quedamos un día en Panama para ver a Raul.
Luego tomamos AIR panama para ir a david. Compramos AIR panama porque el aeropuerto de Albrook estaba más cerca de la ciudad (y del hotel). Sin embargo en AIR panama solo te dejan llevar 1 maleta de mano ya que los aviones son más pequeños.

Porque paramos en Panamá tuve que comprar boleto de ida y vuelta boston-panama-boston y compre separado Air panama para ir a david y copa para regresar a Panama.  El problem de esto fue que tuve que sacar las maletas en Panamá y volver a meterlas para el viaje a Boston.

Otra cosa que tomar en cuenta es que al llegar a Panamá de David tuve que tomar un shuttle para llegar a la terminal en dónde nos dan las maletas.

******* Recomendación
- No tomar AIR panama. Tomar copa de esa manera no hay lio con las maletas de mano.
- Usar multidestino para que las maletas lleguen a donde se supone que deben llegar.

****** Destinos
La estadía en Volcán fue la mejor. Alquile un carro y manejé a Bambito. El camino no fue tan difícil. Sin embargo preferiría no tener que usar carro alquilado por el lio de tener que estar regresando el carro etc. Pero si es necesario tener carro.

Nos quedamos 1 día en Bambito y fue muy agradable. Es bonito, cómodo, la comida es muy buena y hay varias rutas que se pueden tomar para explorar (hiking). La vista es muy buena.


Tambien nos quedamos varios dias en Boquete en Rebequet pero fue muy deceptinante. NO volveremos a ese hotel. La próxima vez es mejor quedarse en Panamonte o algún otro hotel bueno (tal vel Lerida?)
Panamonte tiene la ventaja que es bonito. la comida esta buena, y esta cerca de la ciudad.

Lerida esta muy recomendado pero esta lejos y el camino es malo hasta para manejar.

En boquete no se puede uno confiar de los taxis (no hay terminal) asi que no se va a tener carro entonces uno se debe quedar cerca de la ciudad y no puede ser durante la temporada lluviosa. De repente para la feria de las flores.

Hay varias selecciones de restaurantes buenos en Boquete
- Panamonte para desayuno y cena
- the rock para cena
- OLga para desayuno.
Algunos luegar no tuve tiempo de ir:
- kotoba cafe en "mirador" a la entrada de boquete que podria haber sido una buena vista.
- pastelería alemana.

Tambien el prof moral sugirió ladera hotel.

De repente nos debemos quedar más dias en Panamá capital ya que con subway hace las cosas más fáciles. Definitivamente hay más opciones de restaurante alla.* Projects

*** substraction
#+begin_src R
library(tidyverse)
N = 100
xx = sample(seq(0, 90, 10), N, replace = T)
yy = sample(0:9, N, replace = T)
cards = tibble(ope=paste0(xx, "-", yy, "="),
               val=xx - yy
               )
cards=cards %>% filter(val>=0)'
colnames(cards) = NULL

write.table(cards, col.names = F, row.names = F, file = "~/Onedrive/substraction.txt")

#+end_src
*** basic math facts
#+begin_src R
library(tidyverse)
N = 30
xx = sample(0:9, N, replace = T)
yy = sample(0:9, N, replace = T)
cards = tibble(q=paste0(xx, "+", yy, "="),
               a=xx + yy
               )

N=30
xx = sample(0:20, N, replace = T)
yy = sample(0:9, N, replace = T)
cards2 = tibble(q=ifelse(xx>yy,paste0(xx, "-", yy, "="),
                      paste0(yy, "-", xx, "=")),
               a=abs(xx- yy)
               )


allcards=bind_rows(cards,cards2)
colnames(allcards) = NULL
write.table(allcards, col.names = F, row.names = F, file = "~/Onedrive/Documents/alice/school/basics_facts.txt")

#+end_src

getBill = function(date = "2017-08-01"){
      #library(tidyverse)
      data.table::fread(
	  "./bills/allbills.csv",
	  data.table = F,
	  skip = 2
      ) %>%

* Susan
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       9754cc51-ecce-4291-82a6-4b2db6a65a7d
:END:
** selection
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       36CBB04A-5419-4CB4-B50F-00CCC0232ED7
:END:
#+begin_src R
library(tidyverse)
library(data.table)
df=fread("~/Downloads/cmps-275-jury-results editby SL.csv",data.table=F)
sel1=df %>% group_by(Entry) %>% arrange(desc(Total)) %>% slice(1) %>% arrange(desc(Total)) %>%head(61) %>% as.data.frame()

sel2=df %>% filter(Entry%in% sel1$Entry,
              !Image%in% sel1$Image) %>%
  arrange(desc(Total),Variance) %>% group_by(Entry) %>% slice(1) %>% arrange(desc(Total),Variance) %>%
  head(16)


totaldf=bind_rows(sel1 %>%
          mutate(Selection='first'),
          sel2 %>%
          mutate(Selection="second"))

fwrite(totaldf,file="~/Downloads/cmps_selection.csv")


sel3=df %>% group_by(Entry) %>% arrange(desc(Total)) %>% slice(1) %>% arrange(desc(Total)) %>%filter(Total>=160) %>% as.data.frame()

totaldf2=bind_rows(sel3 %>%
          mutate(Selection='first'),
          sel2 %>%
          mutate(Selection="second"))
fwrite(totaldf2,file="~/Downloads/cmps_selection3.csv")


df %>%
  mutate(Score=Total/Variance) %>%
  filter(grepl("fiesta",Title,ignore.case=T))

df %>%
  mutate(Ratio=Total/Variance,
         Mean=map_dbl(1:nrow(df),function(ii)mean(c(Juror1[ii],Juror2[ii]))),
         SD=map_dbl(1:nrow(df),function(ii)sd(c(Juror1[ii],Juror2[ii]))),
         Min=map_dbl(1:nrow(df),function(ii)min(c(Juror1[ii],Juror2[ii]))),
         Max=map_dbl(1:nrow(df),function(ii)max(c(Juror1[ii],Juror2[ii]))),
         Ratio_mean_sd=Mean/SD) %>%
  group_by(Entry) %>%
  arrange(desc(Total)) %>%
  slice(1) %>%
  arrange(desc(Min),desc(Max)) %>% DT::datatable()

dft=df %>%
  mutate(Ratio=(Total)/Variance,
         Rank1=rank(-Juror1,ties.method='min'),
         Rank2=rank(-Juror2,ties.method='min'),
         MeanRank=(Rank1+Rank2)/2,
         SDRank=map_dbl(1:nrow(df),function(ii)sd(c(Rank1[ii],Rank2[ii]))
                        )
         )

selratio1=dft %>%
  filter(Total>=160) %>%
  group_by(Entry) %>%
  arrange(desc(Total),Variance) %>%
  slice(1) %>%
  arrange(desc(Ratio)) %>%
  head(63)


selratio2=dft %>%
  filter(Total>=160) %>%
  filter(Entry%in% selratio1$Entry[1:62],
              !Image%in% selratio1$Image[1:62]) %>%
  arrange(desc(Total),Variance) %>% group_by(Entry) %>% slice(1) %>% arrange(desc(Ratio)) %>%
  head(17)

total_final=bind_rows(
  selratio1 %>% mutate(Selection='first'),
  selratio2 %>% mutate(Selection="second")
)

total_final %>% DT::datatable()
fwrite(total_final,file="~/Downloads/cmps_selection_ratio2_with_ranks.csv")

df2=fread("~/Onedrive/Documents/susan/awards/cmps_selection_rank_final 071322.csv",data.table=F)

dftot=df2 %>%
  mutate(Rank1=rank(-Juror1,ties.method='min'),
         Rank2=rank(-Juror2,ties.method='min'),
         MeanRank=(Rank1+Rank2)/2,
         SDRank=map_dbl(1:nrow(df2),function(ii)sd(c(Rank1[ii],Rank2[ii]))),
         ToRemove=ifelse(SDRank>40&MeanRank>90|Rank1>150|Rank2>150,1,0)
         )

dftot2=dftot %>%
  filter(ToRemove==0) %>%
  dplyr::select(-ToRemove)

ranksel1= dftot2%>%
  group_by(Entry) %>%
  arrange(MeanRank,SDRank) %>%
  slice(1) %>%
  arrange(MeanRank,SDRank) %>%
  head(62)

ranksel1 %>% DT::datatable()

ranksel2 = dftot2 %>%
  group_by(Entry) %>%
  arrange(MeanRank,SDRank) %>%
  filter(Entry%in% ranksel1$Entry[1:62],
         !Image%in% ranksel1$Image[1:62]) %>%
  arrange(MeanRank,SDRank) %>% group_by(Entry) %>% slice(1) %>%
  arrange(MeanRank) %>%
  head(17)

totaldf_rank=bind_rows(
  ranksel1 %>%ungroup() %>%
  mutate(Selection=rep('first',nrow(ranksel1)),
         Order=1:(nrow(ranksel1))
         ),
  ranksel2 %>%ungroup() %>%
  mutate(Selection="second",
         Order=1:nrow(ranksel2)
         )
)

totaldf_rank %>% DT::datatable()

fwrite(totaldf_rank,file="~/Onedrive/Documents/susan/awards/cmps_selection_rank_final_fixed.csv")
fwrite(dftot,file="~/Onedrive/Documents/susan/awards/cmps_total.csv")


df2=fread("~/Onedrive/Documents/susan/awards/cmps_selection_rank_final 071322.csv",data.table=F)

dftot3=df2 %>%
  mutate(
    Variance=Juror2-Juror1,
    Juror1std=(Juror1-mean(Juror1))/sd(Juror1),
         Juror2std=(Juror2-mean(Juror2))/sd(Juror2),
         MeanScore=map_dbl(1:nrow(df2),function(ii)mean(c(Juror1std[ii],Juror2std[ii]))),
         SDScore=map_dbl(1:nrow(df2),function(ii)sd(c(Juror1std[ii],Juror2std[ii])))
         )

stdsel1=dftot3 %>%
  group_by(Entry) %>%
  arrange(desc(MeanScore),SDScore) %>%
  slice(1) %>%
  arrange(desc(MeanScore),SDScore) %>%
  head(62)

stdsel2=dftot3 %>%
  filter(Entry%in% stdsel1$Entry[1:62],
              !Image%in% stdsel1$Image[1:62]) %>%
  arrange(desc(MeanScore),SDScore) %>%
  group_by(Entry) %>% slice(1) %>%

         MeanRank=(Rank1+Rank2)/2,

         ) %>%

dftot2 = dftot %>% group_by(UserID) %>%
  group_modify( ~ (function(df){
    maxval = min(df$MeanRank)
    totalmax = sum(df$MeanRank == maxval)
    if (totalmax > 1)
      df$tie = T
    else
      df$tie = F
  return(df)
  })(.)) %>% ungroup()

** new version generating new pages.
https://stackoverflow.com/questions/54704901/writing-word-documents-with-the-officer-package-how-to-combine-several-rdocx-ob
use body_add_docx
you need to save each award to disk first then use body_add_docx to merge that file

** award v2
#+begin_src R
library(tidyverse)
library(officer)
setwd("/Users/fred/OneDrive/Documents/susan/certificates")
##library(readxl)
library(data.table)
template_file="awards certificates 2022 one page template correctedv2.docx"
namesfile="psnh22-open-287-awarded-image-list for Susanne reconciled to certificate 101722.csv"
##namesdf=read_excel(namesfile)
namesdf=fread(namesfile,data.table=FALSE)

namesdf %>% head
namesdf %>% tail

fill_info=function(namesdf,ii,template,tmpdir){
  if(!dir.exists(tmpdir))
    dir.create(tmpdir)
  fname=file.path(tmpdir,paste0("winner_",ii,".docx"))
  doc=read_docx(template_file)
  doc=cursor_begin(doc)
  doc=cursor_reach(doc,"XXX")
  nname=paste0("name",ii)
  doc=body_bookmark(doc,nname)
  body_replace_text_at_bkm(doc,nname,namesdf$Award[ii])
  doc=cursor_begin(doc)
  doc=cursor_reach(doc,"XXX")
  nartist=paste0("artist",ii)
  doc=body_bookmark(doc,nartist)
  body_replace_text_at_bkm(doc,nartist,paste0(namesdf[["First Name"]][ii]," ", namesdf[["Last Name"]][ii]))
  doc=cursor_begin(doc)
  doc=cursor_reach(doc,"XXX")
  nawork=paste0("work",ii)
  doc=body_bookmark(doc,nawork)
  body_replace_text_at_bkm(doc,nawork,namesdf[["Title"]][ii])
  print(doc,target=fname)
  return(fname)
}


for(ii in 1:nrow(namesdf)){
  fname=fill_info(namesdf,ii,template,"./tmp/")
  if(ii==1)
    finaldoc=read_docx(fname)
  else{
    finaldoc=body_add_docx(finaldoc,src=fname)
  }
}
print(finaldoc,"final_doc_v2.docx")



#+end_src

## from copilot
ensure_docx_parts <- function(src_docx, template_docx) {
  stopifnot(file.exists(src_docx), file.exists(template_docx))
  src_dir <- tempfile("src_unzip_")
  tpl_dir <- tempfile("tpl_unzip_")
  dir.create(src_dir); dir.create(tpl_dir)
  unzip(src_docx, exdir = src_dir)
  unzip(template_docx, exdir = tpl_dir)
  # list of parts commonly required when merging
  required_parts <- c(
    "word/numbering.xml",
    "word/footnotes.xml",
    "word/endnotes.xml",
    "_rels/.rels",
    "[Content_Types].xml",
    "word/_rels/document.xml.rels"
  )
  for (part in required_parts) {
    src_path <- file.path(src_dir, part)
    tpl_path <- file.path(tpl_dir, part)
    if (!file.exists(src_path) && file.exists(tpl_path)) {
      dir.create(dirname(src_path), recursive = TRUE, showWarnings = FALSE)
      file.copy(tpl_path, src_path, overwrite = TRUE)
    }
  }
  # Recreate the docx by zipping the entire contents of src_dir
  fixed_docx <- tempfile(fileext = ".docx")
  cwd <- getwd()
  on.exit(setwd(cwd), add = TRUE)
  setwd(src_dir)
  files_to_zip <- list.files(".", recursive = TRUE)
  # Ensure files_to_zip is not empty
  if (length(files_to_zip) == 0) stop("Unzipped docx folder is empty: ", src_dir)
  utils::zip(zipfile = fixed_docx, files = files_to_zip)
  return(fixed_docx)
}

finaldoc <- NULL
for(ii in seq_len(nrow(namesdf))) {
  fname <- fill_info(namesdf, ii, template, "./tmp/")
  fname_fixed <- ensure_docx_parts(fname, template_file)
  if (ii == 1) {
    finaldoc <- read_docx(fname)
  } else {
    finaldoc <- body_import_docx(finaldoc, src = fname_fixed)
  }
  # ensure a page break between certificates if needed
  if (ii < nrow(namesdf)) {
    finaldoc <- body_add_break(finaldoc)
  }
}
print(finaldoc, target = "all_certificates_2025_NH.docx")
#+end_src

#+begin_src org
Moderna is seeking a Principal Scientist to join a highly collaborative, multi-disciplinary, fast-paced team based in Cambridge, MA. Ion this role, you will benchmark and develop novel algorithms to incorporate data from diverse modalities in the context of individualized neoantigen therapy (INT, previously known as personalized cancer vaccine, PCV) development. You will collaborate with computational biologists, immunologists, translational scientists, and machine learning scientists to drive INT discovery research and clinical development.


Here’s What You’ll Do


Contribute to evaluations of immunoinformatics algorithms used in T-cell epitope prediction and vaccine design
Develop new algorithms and improve on state-of-the-art algorithms for antigen-based cancer immunotherapy design
Collaborate with computational and wet-lab scientists to support cancer vaccine research and clinical development
Establish and lead innovative best practices for high-dimensional data integration, prediction algorithm development, and rapid iteration with wet-lab collaborators to improve data and model quality


Here’s What You’ll Need (Minimum Qualifications)


Ph.D. in a computational discipline, such as Bioinformatics, Computational Biology, Physics, Computer Science, or Statistics
A minimum of 4 years post-PhD work experience in a computational setting
Demonstrated scientific research experience with algorithm development and machine learning, especially deep learning architectures
Experience with at least one scripting language e.g Python, R and cloud computing, e.g. AWS, GCP


Here’s What You’ll Bring to the Table (Preferred Qualifications)


Prior experience leading the development and productization of prediction algorithms, ideally in the bioinformatics space
Prior experience independently staging and sequencing projects and presenting results to non-technical stakeholders
Strong problem-solving skills and a results-oriented mindset
Outstanding communication skills (verbal and written)
Demonstrated cross-functional collaboration with multidisciplinary teams
Curiosity about exploring a new pathway for drug discovery
Be bold in proposing and delivering on creative experimental designs and ideas
#+end_src

** translation susan
   Hi Heidy
Happy International Women's Day! I did some imaginary paintings lately. See attached.

Hola Heidy
Feliz día internacional de la mujer! Recientemente hice unas pinturas imaginarias. Mira aquí:


This is an imaginary painting based on a Chinese legend known as the "Weaver Girl and the Cowherd".  The original legend is about a love story of a goddess (from the star Vega) and a mortal (from the star Altair),  who are separated by the Milky Way. They can only meet once a year on a bridge built by magpies on July 7th of the lunar calendar, which is known as the Qixi Festival.
In my version, it is about celebrating the intermixing of eastern and western cultures.  Here, in the metaphor of tango dancing, the bridge is bringing together people from different worlds to create something beautiful.

Esta es una pintura imaginaria basada en una leyenda china llamada "El vaquero y la tejedora". La leyenda original es sobre una historia de amor entre una diosa (de la estrella Vega) y un mortal  (de la estrella Altair), quienes estan separados por la Vía Láctea. Solo pueden reunirse una vez al año en un puente construido por urracas en Julio 7 del calendario lunar, festival conocido como el Festival de Qixi.
En mi version, quiero celebrar la mezcla entre las culturas orientales y occidentales. Aqui, en la metáfora del baile de tango, el puente connecta a gentes de diferentes mundos para crear algo hermozo.


This recent piece is loosely based on the Chinese legend of the Moon Goddess (嫦娥) who flew to the moon, except that the flying lady here is wearing a Panamanian costume.  A full moon is often a symbol of a family reunion in Chinese culture. This alludes to what the chinese poet, Su Shi (蘇軾), has quoted in his Water Melody  poem(水調歌頭), "Though thousand miles apart, we can always share the beauty of the moon"(千里共嬋娟).

Here, the child is of a mixed Chinese and Panamanian heritage. She is wondering about her families afar...

Esta obra reciente esta vagamente basada en la legenda china de la diosa de la Luna quien viajo a la luna, excepto que es esta version la diosa esta vestida en un traje Panameño. La luna llena es   frecuentemente usado como símbolo de reunión familiar en la cultura china. Aqui se alude a lo que el poeta chino , Su Shi, dijo en su poema "La Melodía de Agua": "Aunque estemos separados por miles de millas, siempre podemos compartir la belleza de la luna".

* Simcausal debug
#+begin_src R
library("simcausal")
D <- DAG.empty()
D <- D + node("I", distr = "rcat.b1", probs = c(0.1, 0.2, 0.2, 0.2, 0.1, 0.1, 0.1))
Dset <- set.DAG(D,verbose=T)

D <- DAG.empty()
D <- D +
  node("race",
        distr = "rcat.b0",
       probs = c(0.5, 0.25, 0.25))
Dset <- set.DAG(D)

  node("W1",
        distr = "rnorm",
        mean = ifelse(race == 1, 0, ifelse(race == 2, 3, 10)),
        sd = 1) +
  node("W2",
        distr = "runif",
        min = 0, max = 1) +
  node("W3",
        distr = "rbern",
        prob = plogis(-0.5 + 0.7 * W1 + 0.3 * W2)) +
  node("Anode",
        distr = "rbern",
        prob = plogis(-0.5 - 0.3 * W1 - 0.3 * W2 - 0.2 * W3)) +
  node("Y",
        distr = "rbern",
        prob = plogis(-0.1 + 1.2 * Anode + 0.1 * W1 + 0.3 * W2 + 0.2 * W3))
Dset <- set.DAG(D)
#+end_src

#+begin_src R
library(ROCR)
library(bnlearn)

modelstring = paste0("[HIST|LVF][CVP|LVV][PCWP|LVV][HYP][LVV|HYP:LVF][LVF]",
  "[STKV|HYP:LVF][ERLO][HRBP|ERLO:HR][HREK|ERCA:HR][ERCA][HRSA|ERCA:HR][ANES]",
  "[APL][TPR|APL][ECO2|ACO2:VLNG][KINK][MINV|INT:VLNG][FIO2][PVS|FIO2:VALV]",
  "[SAO2|PVS:SHNT][PAP|PMB][PMB][SHNT|INT:PMB][INT][PRSS|INT:KINK:VTUB][DISC]",
  "[MVS][VMCH|MVS][VTUB|DISC:VMCH][VLNG|INT:KINK:VTUB][VALV|INT:VLNG][ACO2|VALV]",
  "[CCHL|ACO2:ANES:SAO2:TPR][HR|CCHL][CO|HR:STKV][BP|CO:TPR]")
true.dag = model2network(modelstring)

strength = boot.strength(alarm, R = 200, m = 30, algorithm = "hc")
pred = as.prediction(strength, true.dag)

performance(pred, "auc") %>% str
performance(pred, "aucpr") %>% str

library(minet)
select=dplyr::select
## get estimated adjacency matrix
est_edges=strength  %>% mutate(weights=strength*direction) %>% select(from,to,weights)
est_ig=igraph::graph_from_data_frame(est_edges)

est_adj= est_ig%>% igraph::get.adjacency(attr="weights") %>% as.matrix()
## get true adjacency matrix
true_adj=igraph::get.adjacency(as.igraph(true.dag)) %>% as.matrix

## make sure nodes are ordered the same way
est_adj=est_adj[colnames(true_adj),colnames(true_adj)]


compa=validate(est_adj,true_adj)
auc.roc(compa)
auc.pr(compa)
#+end_src

email:
Hi Boris, I've been playing with the code and have one quick comment:
- in the assess function you are using the bnlearn function as.prediction  to calcualte the convert the edge list to get the predictions and then using the ROCR. Unfortunately that function as.prediction is not to be trusted. It is not using all edges but a subset and it is not even clear how that subset is selected. If you take a look at the source code you will see. I brought this up with Scutari not too long ago and he said that he will be deprecating and removing that function in the future. I will forward you my email conversation with him on this topic. Instead I recommend you to use minet which uses adjacency matrix instead. In this email there is an example.

- I tried my own model based on the insurance network which have 27 nodes and got: "Error in private$calculateLatVars(res, method = latentSpaceMethod, scale = scale,  :  Latent space not identified - aborting"
  - do you have an insight on how many nodes you need to have in order to be able to determine the latent confounder?

** write distribution plotter
#+begin_src R
library(shiny)
library(ggplot2)

# Define a function to compute the density values for a given distribution and parameter values
ddist <- function(x, dist, params) {
  do.call(density, list(x = x, bw = "SJ", kernel = "gaussian", from = qmin(dist), to = qmax(dist), weights = do.call(dist, c(list(x), params))))$y
}

# Define a function to compute the minimum quantile value for a given distribution
qmin <- function(dist) {
  switch(dist,
    "beta" = 0,
    "binomial" = 0,
    "cauchy" = -Inf,
    "chi-squared" = 0,
    "exponential" = 0,
    "f" = 0,
    "gamma" = 0,
    "geometric" = 0,
    "hypergeometric" = 0,
    "log-normal" = 0,
    "negative binomial" = 0,
    "normal" = -Inf,
    "Poisson" = 0,
    "t" = -Inf,
    "uniform" = input$min,
    "weibull" = 0
  )
}

# Define a function to compute the maximum quantile value for a given distribution
qmax <- function(dist) {
  switch(dist,
    "beta" = 1,
    "binomial" = input$size,
    "cauchy" = Inf,
    "chi-squared" = Inf,
    "exponential" = Inf,
    "f" = Inf,
    "gamma" = Inf,
    "geometric" = Inf,
    "hypergeometric" = input$k,
    "log-normal" = Inf,
    "negative binomial" = Inf,
    "normal" = Inf,
    "Poisson" = Inf,
    "t" = Inf,
    "uniform" = input$max,
    "weibull" = Inf
  )
}

# Define the UI
ui <- fluidPage(
  titlePanel("Probability Distribution Viewer"),
  sidebarLayout(
    sidebarPanel(
      selectInput("dist", "Distribution:",
        choices = c("beta", "binomial", "cauchy", "chi-squared", "exponential", "f", "gamma", "geometric", "hypergeometric", "log-normal", "negative binomial", "normal", "Poisson", "t", "uniform", "weibull"),
        selected = "normal"
      ),
      sliderInput("min", "Minimum value:", min = -10, max = 10, value = -3, step = 0.1),
      sliderInput("max", "Maximum value:", min = -10, max = 10, value = 3, step = 0.1),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'beta'",
        sliderInput("shape1", "Shape parameter 1:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 2),
        sliderInput("shape2", "Shape parameter 2:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 2)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'binomial'",
        sliderInput("size", "Number of trials:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 10)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'cauchy'",
        sliderInput("location", "Location parameter:", min = -10, max = 10, value = 0),
        sliderInput("scale", "Scale parameter:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'chi-squared'",
        sliderInput("df", "Degrees of freedom:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 10)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'exponential'",
        sliderInput("rate", "Rate parameter:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'f'",
        sliderInput("df1", "Degrees of freedom 1:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 10),
        sliderInput("df2", "Degrees of freedom 2:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 10)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'gamma'",
        sliderInput("shape", "Shape parameter:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 2),
        sliderInput("rate", "Rate parameter:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'geometric'",
        sliderInput("prob", "Success probability:", min = 0.1, max = 1, value = 0.5)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'hypergeometric'",
        sliderInput("m", "Number of objects in population:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 20),
        sliderInput("n", "Number of objects in sample:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 10),
        sliderInput("k", "Number of objects of interest in population:", min = 1, max = input$m, value = 5)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'log-normal'",
        sliderInput("meanlog", "Mean of the underlying normal distribution in log scale:", min = -10, max = 10, value = 0),
        sliderInput("sdlog", "Standard deviation of the underlying normal distribution in log scale:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'negative binomial'",
        sliderInput("size", "Number of failures before stopping:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 10),
        sliderInput("prob", "Success probability:", min = 0.1, max = 1, value = 0.5)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'normal'",
        sliderInput("mean", "Mean:", min = -10, max = 10, value = 0),
        sliderInput("sd", "Standard deviation:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'Poisson'",
        sliderInput("lambda", "Rate parameter:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 't'",
        sliderInput("df", "Degrees of freedom:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 10)
      ),
      conditionalPanel(
        condition = "input.dist == 'uniform'",
        sliderInput("min", "Minimum value:", min = -10, max = 0, value = -5),
        sliderInput("max", "Maximum value:", min = 0, max = 10, value = 5)
      )
    ),
    mainPanel(
      plotOutput("distPlot")
    )
  )
)

server <- function(input, output) {
  output$distPlot <- renderPlot({
    # Define x values to plot
    ##x <- seq(qmin[input$dist](), qmax[input$dist](), length.out = 100)
    x=seq(input$min,input$max,length.out=100)
    # Generate distribution values based on user input
    y <- ddist[input$dist](x,
      location = input$location,
      scale = input$scale,
      df = input$df,
      shape = input$shape,
      rate = input$rate,
      prob = input$prob,
      m = input$m,
      n = input$n,
      k = input$k,
      meanlog = input$meanlog,
      sdlog = input$sdlog,
      size = input$size,
      mean = input$mean,
      sd = input$sd,
      lambda = input$lambda)

    # Plot distribution using ggplot
    ggplot() +
      geom_line(aes(x, y), color = "blue") +
      ggtitle(input$dist)
  })
}

shinyApp(ui = ui, server = server)
#+end_src

** write R code using torch R package to creat a variational autoencoder
#+begin_src R
# Load the necessary libraries
library(reticulate)
reticulate::use_condaenv("pytorch")
library(torch)
library(dplyr)

torch::cuda_is_available()
device <- ifelse(torch::cuda_is_available(), "cuda", "cpu")
device

# Define the dimensions of the input and latent variables
input_size <- 784 # 28x28 MNIST images
hidden_size <- 256
latent_size <- 2

# Define the encoder network
encoder <- nn_module(
  nn_linear(input_size, hidden_size),
  nn_relu(),
  nn_linear(hidden_size, latent_size*2) # double the size to get mean and log variance
)

# Define the decoder network
decoder <- nn_module(
  nn_linear(latent_size, hidden_size),
  nn_relu(),
  nn_linear(hidden_size, input_size),
  nn_sigmoid() # output is in range [0,1] like MNIST images
)

# Define the VAE model
vae <- nn_module(
  nn_input(),
  encoder,
  nn_flatten(),
  nn_module(
    nn_linear(latent_size, latent_size),
    nn_linear(latent_size, latent_size)
  ),
  nn_flatten(),
  decoder
)

# Move the model to the device
vae$to(device)

# Define the loss function
vae_loss <- function(x, x_recon, mu, log_var) {
  # Reconstruction loss
  recon_loss <- nn_binary_cross_entropy(x_recon, x, reduction = "mean")

  # KL divergence loss
  kl_loss <- -0.5 * torch::sum(1 + log_var - mu^2 - torch::exp(log_var))

  # Total loss
  total_loss <- recon_loss + kl_loss

  return(total_loss)
}

# Define the optimizer
optimizer <- optim_adam(vae$parameters(), lr = 1e-3)

# Define the training loop
train_vae <- function(model, dataloader, optimizer, device, epochs) {
  for (epoch in 1:epochs) {
    for (i in 1:length(dataloader)) {
      # Get the data batch and move to device
      data <- dataloader[[i]]$to(device)

      # Reset the gradients
      optimizer$zero_grad()

      # Forward pass
      output <- model(data)
      x_recon <- output[[1]]
      mu <- output[[2]][, 1:latent_size]
      log_var <- output[[2]][, (latent_size+1):(2*latent_size)]

      # Compute the loss
      loss <- vae_loss(data, x_recon, mu, log_var)

      # Backward pass
      loss$backward()

      # Update the parameters
      optimizer$step()
    }
    cat(sprintf("Epoch %d, Loss: %.4f\n", epoch, loss$item()))
  }
}

# Load the MNIST dataset
mnist <- torch::datasets::mnist("~/data/mnist", train = TRUE, download = TRUE)

# Create a data loader for the MNIST dataset
batch_size <- 128
train_loader <- torch::data::DataLoader(mnist, batch_size = batch_size, shuffle = TRUE)

# Train the VAE
train_vae(vae, train_loader, optimizer, device, epochs = 10)
#+end_src

#+begin_src R
library(shiny)
library(ggplot2)
library(patchwork)

ui <- fluidPage(
  titlePanel("Probability Distribution Plotter"),
  sidebarLayout(
    sidebarPanel(
      selectInput("distribution", "Select a Distribution:",
                  choices = c("beta", "binomial", "cauchy", "chi-squared",
                              "exponential", "f", "gamma", "geometric",
                              "hypergeometric", "log-normal", "negative binomial",
                              "normal", "Poisson", "t", "uniform", "weibull")),
      uiOutput("parameters")
    ),
    mainPanel(
      plotOutput("plot")
    )
  )
)

server <- function(input, output) {
  output$parameters <- renderUI({
    dist <- input$distribution
    switch(dist,
           "beta" = tagList(
             sliderInput("alpha", "Alpha:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1),
             sliderInput("beta", "Beta:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
           ),
           "binomial" = tagList(
             sliderInput("size", "Size:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 10),
             sliderInput("prob", "Prob:", min = 0, max = 1, value = 0.5)
           ),
           "cauchy" = tagList(
             sliderInput("location", "Location:", min = -10, max = 10, value = 0),
             sliderInput("scale", "Scale:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
           ),
           "chi-squared" = tagList(
             sliderInput("df", "Degrees of Freedom:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 5)
           ),
           "exponential" = tagList(
             sliderInput("rate", "Rate:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
           ),
           "f" = tagList(
             sliderInput("df1", "Degrees of Freedom 1:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 5),
             sliderInput("df2", "Degrees of Freedom 2:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 5)
           ),
           "gamma" = tagList(
             sliderInput("shape", "Shape:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1),
             sliderInput("rate", "Rate:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
           ),
           "geometric" = tagList(
             sliderInput("prob", "Prob:", min = 0, max = 1, value = 0.5)
           ),
           "hypergeometric" = tagList(
             sliderInput("m", "M:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 10),
             sliderInput("n", "N:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 20),
             sliderInput("k", "K:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 5)
           ),
           "log-normal" = tagList(
             sliderInput("meanlog", "Mean Log:", min = -10, max = 10, value = 0),
             sliderInput("sdlog", "SD ", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
           ),
           "negative binomial" = tagList(
             sliderInput("size", "Size:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 10),
             sliderInput("prob", "Prob:", min = 0, max = 1, value = 0.5)
           ),
           "normal" = tagList(
             sliderInput("mean", "Mean:", min = -10, max = 10, value = 0),
             sliderInput("sd", "SD:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
           ),
           "Poisson" = tagList(
             sliderInput("lambda", "Lambda:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
           ),
           "t" = tagList(
             sliderInput("df", "Degrees of Freedom:", min = 1, max = 100, value = 5)
           ),
           "uniform" = tagList(
             sliderInput("min", "Min:", min = -10, max = 0, value = -5),
             sliderInput("max", "Max:", min = 0, max = 10, value = 5)
           ),
           "weibull" = tagList(
             sliderInput("shape", "Shape:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1),
             sliderInput("scale", "Scale:", min = 0.1, max = 10, value = 1)
           )
           )
  })
  qmin <- function(dist) {
  switch(dist,
         "beta" = 0,
         "binomial" = 0,
         "cauchy" = -10,
         "chi-squared" = 0,
         "exponential" = 0,
         "f" = 0,
         "gamma" = 0,
         "geometric" = 0,
         "hypergeometric" = 0,
         "log-normal" = 0,
         "negative binomial" = 0,
         "normal" = -10,
         "Poisson" = 0,
         "t" = -10,
         "uniform" = -10,
         "weibull" = 0)
  }
  qmax <- function(dist) {
  switch(dist,
         "beta" = 1,
         "binomial" = input$size,
         "cauchy" = Inf,
         "chi-squared" = Inf,
         "exponential" = Inf,
         "f" = Inf,
         "gamma" = Inf,
         "geometric" = Inf,
         "hypergeometric" = input$k,
         "log-normal" = Inf,
         "negative binomial" = Inf,
         "normal" = Inf,
         "Poisson" = Inf,
         "t" = Inf,
         "uniform" = input$max,
         "weibull" = Inf)
}
                                        # Define a reactive expression to compute the x and y values for the plot
  get_data <- function(dist){
    x <- seq(qmin(dist_fun), qmax(dist_fun), length.out = 1000)
    y <- switch(input$distribution,
              "beta" = dbeta(x, dist_fun$alpha, dist_fun$beta),
              "binomial" = dbinom(x, dist_fun$size, dist_fun$prob),
              "cauchy" = dcauchy(x, dist_fun$location, dist_fun$scale),
              "chi-squared" = dchisq(x, dist_fun$df),
              "exponential" = dexp(x, dist_fun$rate),
              "f" = df(x, dist_fun$df1, dist_fun$df2),
              "gamma" = dgamma(x, dist_fun$shape, dist_fun$rate),
              "geometric" = dgeom(x, dist_fun$prob),
              "hypergeometric" = dhyper(x, dist_fun$m, dist_fun$n, dist_fun$k),
              "log-normal" = dlnorm(x, dist_fun$meanlog, dist_fun$sdlog),
              "negative binomial" = dnbinom(x, dist_fun$size, dist_fun$prob),
              "normal" = dnorm(x, dist_fun$mean, dist_fun$sd),
              "Poisson" = dpois(x, dist_fun$lambda),
              "t" = dt(x, dist_fun$df),
              "uniform" = dunif(x, dist_fun$min, dist_fun$max),
              "weibull" = dweibull(x, dist_fun$shape, dist_fun$scale))
  data.frame(x = x, y = y)
  })

  output$plot <- renderPlot({
    dist <- input$distribution
    params <- switch(dist,
                     "beta" = list(alpha = input$alpha, beta = input$beta),
                     "binomial" = list(size = input$size, prob = input$prob),
                     "cauchy" = list(location = input$location, scale = input$scale),
                     "chi-squared" = list(df = input$df),
                     "exponential" = list(rate = input$rate),
                     "f" = list(df1 = input$df1, df2 = input$df2),
                     "gamma" = list(shape = input$shape, rate = input$rate),
                     "geometric" = list(prob = input$prob),
                     "hypergeometric" = list(m = input$m, n = input$n, k = input$k),
                     "log-normal" = list(meanlog = input$meanlog, sdlog = input$sdlog),
                     "negative binomial" = list(size = input$size, prob = input$prob),
                     "normal" = list(mean = input$mean, sd = input$sd),
                     "Poisson" = list(lambda = input$lambda),
                     "t" = list(df = input$df),
                     "uniform" = list(min = input$min, max = input$max),
                     "weibull" = list(shape = input$shape, scale = input$scale)
                     )
    dist_fun <- do.call(dist, params)
    x <- seq(qmin(dist_fun), qmax(dist_fun), length.out = 1000)
    data <- data.frame(x = x, density = ddist(dist_fun, x))
    ggplot(data, aes(x = x, y = density)) +
      geom_line(size = 1.5) +
      theme_classic() +
      labs(x = "X", y = "Density", title = paste("Probability Distribution Plot -", dist))
  })
}

shinyApp(ui, server)

** reads convertion

Write an interactive elisp function that takes the selected string:
saveRDS(somevariable, file='somepath.RDS')
and converts it to
if(!exists('somevariable'))
  somevariable=readRDS('somepath.RDS')


Here is an example of an interactive elisp function that does this conversion:

#+begin_src elisp
(defun convert-saveRDS-to-readRDS (beg end)
  "Converts saveRDS function call to readRDS function call.
   Takes a selected string as input and converts it to:
   if(!exists('somevariable'))
     somevariable=readRDS('somepath.RDS')"
  (interactive "r")
  (let ((str (buffer-substring-no-properties beg end)))
    (when (string-match "^saveRDS(\\(.*\\), file='\\(.*\\)')" str)
      (let* ((var (match-string 1 str))
             (file-path (match-string 2 str))
             (output (format "if(!exists('%s'))
  %s=readRDS('%s')" var var file-path)))
        (delete-region beg end)
        (insert output)))))
#+end_src

To use this function, select the `saveRDS()` function call in the buffer and run `M-x convert-saveRDS-to-readRDS` (or whatever keybinding you prefer). The function call will be replaced with the converted `if` statement.

** another more general
Write an interactive elisp function that takes the selected string in the general form:
saveRDS(somevariable, file="somepath.RDS")
and converts it to
if(!exists("somevariable"))
  somevariable=readRDS("somepath.RDS")

saveRDS(somevariable, file='somepath.RDS')


Here's an interactive elisp function that takes the selected string and converts it to the desired format:

#+begin_src elisp
(defun convert-saveRDS-to-if-exists ()
  "Converts selected saveRDS string to if exists format."
  (interactive)
  (let ((orig-str (buffer-substring (region-beginning) (region-end)))
        (new-str ""))
    (if (string-match "saveRDS(\\(.*\\),\\s-*file=[\"']\\([^\"']+[.]RDS\\)[\"'])" orig-str)
        (progn
          (setq new-str (concat "if (!exists(\"" (match-string 1 orig-str) "\"))\n"
                                "  " (match-string 1 orig-str) " = readRDS(\"" (match-string 2 orig-str) "\")"))
          (delete-region (region-beginning) (region-end))
          (insert new-str))
      (message "Selected string is not in the correct format."))))
#+end_src

To use this function:

1. Select the `saveRDS` string you want to convert in your buffer.
2. Run the function with `M-x convert-saveRDS-to-if-exists`. This will replace the selected string with the converted version.
3. If the selected string is not in the correct format (i.e. it doesn't match the regular expression), the function will display an error message and do nothing.

Note that this function assumes that the selected string is a complete `saveRDS` call with a filename specified using either single or double quotes. If your `saveRDS` calls are more complex (e.g. with additional arguments), you may need to adjust the regular expression in the function to match the specific format you're working with.

saveRDS(tst, file="mypat.RDS")

saveRDS(somevariable, file="somepath.RDS")

#+begin_ai
in graphviz how do I create group graphs
To create group graphs in Graphviz, you can use the "subgraph" keyword to define the group and then use the "rank" keyword to control the layout of the subgraphs. Here's an example of how to create group graphs in Graphviz:

```dot
digraph {
  // Define main graph attributes
  rankdir=LR;
  node [shape=box];

  // Create subgraph 1
  subgraph cluster_1 {
    label="Group 1";
    style=dotted;
    color=blue;

    A -> B;
    B -> C;
  }

  // Create subgraph 2
  subgraph cluster_2 {
    label="Group 2";
    style=dotted;
    color=red;

    D -> E;
    E -> F;
  }

  // Define edges between groups or nodes
  A -> D;
  C -> F;
}
```

This code will create two group graphs labeled "Group 1" and "Group 2" with edges inside each group. There are also edges between the groups, connecting nodes A to D and C to F. The "style" attribute is used to make the groups dotted, and the "color" attribute sets the color of the groups.

You can customize the appearance and properties of the groups and nodes to fit your specific requirements.

[ME]: how can I create independent networks using the same node names
To create independent networks using the same node names in Graphviz, you can use the "subgraph" keyword to define separate subgraphs within the main graph. Each subgraph can have its own set of nodes with the same names. Here's an example:

```dot
digraph {
  // Create network 1
  subgraph cluster_1 {
    label="Network 1";

    // Nodes for network 1
    A -> B;
    B -> C;
  }

  // Create network 2
  subgraph cluster_2 {
    label="Network 2";

    // Nodes for network 2
    A -> D;
    D -> E;
  }
}
```

In this example, there are two independent networks labeled "Network 1" and "Network 2". Both networks have a node named A, but they are distinct within their own subgraphs. Each network can have its own set of nodes and edges without affecting the other network.

By encapsulating the nodes within separate subgraphs, you can create multiple independent networks with the same node names.

[ME]: in graphviz how can you label the nodes?
To label the nodes in Graphviz, you can use the "label" attribute within the node definition. Here's an example:

```dot
digraph {
  A [label="Node A"];
  B [label="Node B"];
  C [label="Node C"];

  A -> B;
  B -> C;
}
```

In this code, the nodes A, B, and C are defined with the "label" attribute specifying their labels as "Node A," "Node B," and "Node C," respectively. These labels will be displayed within the nodes when the graph is rendered.

You can customize the node labels as per your requirements by setting different strings or values for the "label" attribute.

#+begin_src org
Moderna is seeking a Principal Scientist to join a highly collaborative, multi-disciplinary, fast-paced team based in Cambridge, MA. In this role, you will benchmark and develop novel algorithms to incorporate data from diverse modalities in the context of individualized neoantigen therapy (INT, previously known as personalized cancer vaccine, PCV) development. You will collaborate with computational biologists, immunologists, translational scientists, and machine learning scientists to drive INT discovery research and clinical development.

Here's What You'll Do

- Contribute to evaluations of immunoinformatics algorithms used in T-cell epitope prediction and vaccine design

- Develop new algorithms and improve on state-of-the-art algorithms for antigen-based cancer immunotherapy design

- Collaborate with computational and wet-lab scientists to support cancer vaccine research and clinical development

- Establish and lead innovative best practices for high-dimensional data integration, prediction algorithm development, and rapid it eration with wet-lab collaborators to improve data and model quality

Here's What You'll Need (Minimum Qualifications)

- Ph.D. in a computational discipline, such as Bioinformatics, Computational Biology, Physics, Computer Science, or Statistics

- A minimum of 4 years post-PhD work experience in a computational setting

- Demonstrated scientific research experience with algorithm development and machine learning, especially deep learning architectures

- Experience with at least one scripting language e.g Python, R and cloud computing, e.g. AWS, GCP


Here's What You'll Bring to the Table (Preferred Qualifications)

- Prior experience leading the development and productization of prediction algorithms, ideally in the bioinformatics space

- Prior experience independently staging and sequencing projects and presenting results to non-technical stakeholders

- Strong problem-solving skills and a results-oriented mindset

- Outstanding communication skills (verbal and written)

- Demonstrated cross-functional collaboration with multidisciplinary teams

- Curiosity about exploring a new pathway for drug discovery

- Be bold in proposing and delivering on creative experimental designs and ideas

Moderna is pleased to provide you and your family with a comprehensive and innovative suite of benefits, including:

Highly competitive and inclusive medical, dental and vision coverage options
Flexible Spending Accounts for medical expenses and dependent care expenses
Lifestyle Spending Account funds to help you engage in personal enrichment and self-care activities
Family care benefits, including subsidized back-up care options and on-demand tutoring
Free premium access to fitness, nutrition, and mindfulness classes
Exclusive preferred pricing on Peloton fitness equipment
Adoption and family-planning benefits
Dedicated care coordination support for our LGBTQ+ community
Generous paid time off, including:
    Vacation, sick time and holidays
    Volunteer time to participate within your community
    Discretionary year-end shutdown
    Paid sabbatical after 5 years; every 3 years thereafter
Generous Paid Leave offerings, including 18 weeks of 100% paid parental leave for all new parents
401k match and Financial Planning tools
Moderna-paid Life, LTD and STD insurance coverages, as well as voluntary benefit options
Complimentary concierge service including home services research, travel booking, and entertainment requests
Free parking or subsidized commuter passes
Location-specific perks and extras!
#+end_src

  - general machine learning

      - gower distance
  - Analysis of simulated samples from causal networks
    - Implementation of causal inference methods like double robust treatment effect estimates.

    - clustering of CNV

    - finding genes in from regions of structural variant.

** summary
I have experience on the analysis of variety of oncological datasets for multiple types of cancers including lung cancer, multiple myeloma, prostate cancer, pancreatic, and kidney from different data sources including TCGA/GDC, Flatiron, ICGC, Tempus MMRF, and others. The analysis include data preprocssing, model creation, and model interpretation.

My interest is in the development of statistical and machine learning models applied to medical data with a strong emphasis on causal methods (discovery and inference), high dimensional variable selection and interpretability of the result with applications to predictive modeling, predictive and prognostic biomarkers selection, drug targets discovery, and causal mechanism inference. In my previous projects I have  worked with high dimensional genomic data like RNAseq, CNV, SNV, microarray, translocations as well as clinical variables. My quantitative experience goes beyond the usual machine learning topics (logistic regression, Neural Networks, Support Vector Machine, Genetic Algorithms) to include other techniques like statistical signal processing  (random processes, optimal filters, Bayesian estimation) , inverse problem theory (regularized least square solutions, pseudoinverse), sparse coding (sparse reconstruction, dictionary learning), and simulation and modeling (discrete event simulation, statistical modeling).

As part of my job I also develop and mantain 4 R packages for internal use among my group with diverse algorithms for data preprocessing, visualization, machine learning, and analysis.

Programming: R, Python, Matlab
*** resume version2
Experienced in analyzing oncological datasets from various sources including TCGA/GDC, Flatiron, ICGC, Tempus MMRF, and others. Proficient in data preprocessing, model creation, and model interpretation. * susan
#+begin_src R
tst=fread("~/OneDrive/susan/2023 T2 Input Template GI S62605 YJ 091423 test.csv",data.table=F,na.strings="")

tst2=tst %>% mutate(across(matches("M.*"),as.numeric)) %>%
  mutate(across(c(CC,AN,Project,`Clin. Study`,`Cost Cat.`),function(cc)ifelse(cc=="",'miss',cc))) %>% group_by(CC,AN,Project,`Clin. Study`,`Cost Cat.`) %>% summarise_all(function(xx)sum(xx,na.rm=T))

tst2%>% DT::datatable()

tst %>% DT::datatable()

fwrite(tst2,file="csvfixed.csv")
#+end_src

** Recipes
I would to develop a web app that can work on android or on a desktop. I would like to be able to import recipes and it should process the ingredient section to recognize units  and quantities in term of integers for things like eggs or ML (where I don't want to look for 2.5 mm) or  fractions if it cups.

 I will use org-chef in emacs to create a cookbook.org file with all recipes. See https://github.com/Chobbes/org-chef

 I'm including an example for you to see.

 Ideally i would like to be able to export this cookbook.org in emacs to the html app which I can then serve from a synology drive or some other website server so I can load it in android.

 I want the quantities to be active in the sense that I can adjust any one components and automatically adjust the rest of the ingredients. There should also be an edit mode in case I want to modify the recipe without affecting other ingredients. This mode should modify the orgmode file (cookbook.org)
